# Wesean: The Definitive Master Guide & Dictionary
## A Neutral, Cross-Tribal Bridge Language for Western Southeast Asia (WESEA)

---

### Introduction: The Sociolinguistic Vision
Western Southeast Asia (WESEA) is a region of immense cultural and linguistic diversity. With hundreds of distinct languages spoken across Tibeto-Burman and Austroasiatic families, contact communication has historically relied on external regional languages. This reliance often introduces cultural asymmetry and political friction.

**Wesean** is a constructed contact language (conlang) designed to serve as a neutral cultural and economic bridge. Drawing inspiration from Lee Kuan Yew's neutral language strategy in Singapore, Wesean is designed so that no single tribe, community, or language family enjoys dominance. By stripping away complex grammatical features and establishing a balanced, proportional vocabulary shared equally among the region's main language families, Wesean fosters mutual respect, preserves tribal sovereignty, and facilitates digital trade and community cooperation.

## 1. THE LINGUISTIC BLUEPRINT

Wesean strips away the complex inflectional morphology, grammatical gender systems, and heavy case markings found in Sanskritized languages of the plains (such as Assamese and Hindi). Instead, it adopts a streamlined, highly analytic structure designed for rapid acquisition and unambiguous communication.

### Word Order (Syntax)
Wesean enforces a rigid **Subject-Object-Verb (SOV)** word order. This SOV structure provides immediate typological consistency for speakers of Tibeto-Burman languages, while remaining highly systematic and easily learnable for Austroasiatic speakers.

- **Structure:** `Subject` + `Object` + `Verb`
- **Modifiers:** Adjectives and possessive modifiers follow the nouns they describe.

### The Pronoun Matrix
Pronouns are highly regular and lack gender markers. Plural forms are created systematically by appending the plural particle **tem** (Tani origin) after the singular pronoun.

| Person | Singular (Subject) | Plural (Subject) | Origin & Etymology |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **1st Person** | **anga** (I / me) | **anga tem** (We / us) | Boro-Garo (*ang*) + Tani (*tem*) |
| **2nd Person** | **nang** (You) | **nang tem** (You all) | Karbi (*nang*) + Tani (*tem*) |
| **3rd Person** | **paw** (He / she / it) | **paw tem** (They / them) | Zo (*paw*) + Tani (*tem*) |

### Case Markings & Grammatical Relations
As an analytic language, Wesean does not use suffixal noun cases. Instead, grammatical relations are expressed through word order and separate particles or post-positions:
1. **Genitive (Possession):** Indicated by the particle **ke** (Naga/Konyak origin) placed between the possessor and the possessed.
   - *anga ke yum* (my house)
2. **Locative (Location/Direction):** Expressed using the post-position **lo** (at / in / to; Khasi/Tibeto-Burman origin) or **tin** (from; Tibeto-Burman origin) placed after the noun.
   - *iew lo* (at the market / to the market)
   - *iing tin* (from the house)

### Verb Tense, Aspect, and Negation
Verbs in Wesean are invariant roots that do not conjugate for person, number, or tense. Tense and mood are indicated by post-verbal particles:
- **Present Tense (Continuous/General):** Indicated by the bare verb, or marked with **du** (Tangkhul origin) for active, ongoing actions.
- **Past Tense:** Marked by the post-verbal particle **to** (Ao origin).
- **Future Tense:** Marked by the post-verbal particle **po** (Rongmei origin).
- **Negation:** Marked by the particle **ma** (Mishmi origin), placed immediately after the tense particle (or after the verb if in the present tense).

---

### Grammatical Demonstration & Sentence Construction

Below are five standard sentences demonstrating Wesean syntax, grammatical particles, and vocabulary, along with their word-by-word morphological breakdowns and natural English translations.

#### Sentence 1: General Present Action
- **Wesean:** *Anga nang iew lo yengba du.*
- **Morphological Breakdown:**
  | Word | Wesean Term | Meaning / Grammatical Gloss | Origin |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | 1 | Anga | I (1st Person Singular Subject) | Boro-Garo |
  | 2 | nang | you (2nd Person Singular Object) | Karbi |
  | 3 | iew | market | Khasi |
  | 4 | lo | at / in (Locative Post-position) | Khasi |
  | 5 | yengba | see / look at | Meitei |
  | 6 | du | [PRES] (Present Active Particle) | Tangkhul |
- **English Translation:** I see you at the market.

#### Sentence 2: Past Action with Direct Object
- **Wesean:** *Paw tang bre to.*
- **Morphological Breakdown:**
  | Word | Wesean Term | Meaning / Grammatical Gloss | Origin |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | 1 | Paw | He / She (3rd Person Singular Subject) | Zo |
  | 2 | tang | rice / grain | Zo |
  | 3 | bre | buy | Boro-Garo |
  | 4 | to | [PAST] (Past Tense Particle) | Ao |
- **English Translation:** She bought the rice.

#### Sentence 3: Future Movement with Locative Direction
- **Wesean:** *Anga tem nam lo tlan po.*
- **Morphological Breakdown:**
  | Word | Wesean Term | Meaning / Grammatical Gloss | Origin |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | 1 | Anga | I (1st Person Singular) | Boro-Garo |
  | 2 | tem | [PL] (Plural Particle) | Tani |
  | 3 | nam | village | Tani |
  | 4 | lo | to / towards (Locative Post-position) | Khasi |
  | 5 | tlan | run | Zo |
  | 6 | po | [FUT] (Future Tense Particle) | Rongmei |
- **English Translation:** We will run to the village.

#### Sentence 4: Negated Present Action
- **Wesean:** *Nang laika lo thoh ma.*
- **Morphological Breakdown:**
  | Word | Wesean Term | Meaning / Grammatical Gloss | Origin |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | 1 | Nang | You (2nd Person Singular Subject) | Karbi |
  | 2 | laika | book / record | Jingphaw |
  | 3 | lo | in / at (Locative Post-position) | Khasi |
  | 4 | thoh | write | Khasi |
  | 5 | ma | [NEG] (Negative Particle) | Mishmi |
- **English Translation:** You do not write in the book.

#### Sentence 5: Negated Past Action with Liquid Concept
- **Wesean:** *Paw tem di-alo rao to ma.*
- **Morphological Breakdown:**
  | Word | Wesean Term | Meaning / Grammatical Gloss | Origin |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | 1 | Paw | He / She (3rd Person Singular) | Zo |
  | 2 | tem | [PL] (Plural Particle) | Tani |
  | 3 | di-alo | brine (di- [liquid] + alo [salt]) | Boro-Garo + Tani |
  | 4 | rao | gather / take / obtain | Zo |
  | 5 | to | [PAST] (Past Tense Particle) | Ao |
  | 6 | ma | [NEG] (Negative Particle) | Mishmi |
- **English Translation:** They did not gather the salt-water.

#### The Liquid Prefix Rule (`di-`)
A unique orthographic and structural rule governs all fluid and liquid concepts in Wesean. All liquid, water, and fluid terms must exclusively use the Boro-Garo prefix **di-**.
- *di-nai* (river water; *di-* [liquid] + *nai* [river root])
- *di-alo* (salt-water / brine; *di-* [liquid] + *alo* [salt])
- *di-gwi* (lava; *di-* [liquid] + *gwi* [fire])
- *di-hna* (sweat / perspiration; *di-* [liquid] + *hna* [face])

### 15 Essential Trade and Cooperation Phrases
The following everyday phrases are optimized for business, trading, digital e-commerce, and community negotiations.

#### Phrase 1: Inquiring about Price
- **Wesean:** *Doh soh ke dor aei?*
- **Breakdown:** *doh* [this] *soh* [fruit] *ke* [of] *dor* [price] *aei* [what]
- **English Translation:** What is the price of this fruit?

#### Phrase 2: Placing an Order
- **Wesean:** *Anga lai kilo tang bre zaw.*
- **Breakdown:** *Anga* [I] *lai* [three] *kilo* [kilo] *tang* [rice] *bre* [buy] *zaw* [want]
- **English Translation:** I would like to buy three kilograms of rice.

#### Phrase 3: Specifying Payment Quality
- **Wesean:** *Doh sel hao qian.*
- **Breakdown:** *doh* [this] *sel* [coin] *hao* [good/pure] *qian* [money]
- **English Translation:** This coin is made of pure silver.

#### Phrase 4: Professional Trading Etiquette
- **Wesean:** *Anga tem hao lam die-bre.*
- **Breakdown:** *Anga tem* [we] *hao* [good] *lam* [way/manner] *die-bre* [sell-buy / trade]
- **English Translation:** We should conduct our business with mutual respect.

#### Phrase 5: Seeking Marketplace Directions
- **Wesean:** *Nam ke iew lo aei?*
- **Breakdown:** *nam* [village] *ke* [of] *iew* [market] *lo* [at] *aei* [where]
- **English Translation:** Where is the local market located?

#### Phrase 6: Reporting Transaction Discrepancies
- **Wesean:** *Paw dor lut to ma.*
- **Breakdown:** *Paw* [he] *dor* [price] *lut* [pay] *to* [PAST] *ma* [NEG]
- **English Translation:** He did not pay the agreed price.

#### Phrase 7: Travel Safety Inquiry
- **Wesean:** *Nam ke lam hao?*
- **Breakdown:** *nam* [town] *ke* [of] *lam* [road] *hao* [good/safe]
- **English Translation:** Is the road leading to the town safe to travel?

#### Phrase 8: Inspecting Inventory
- **Wesean:** *Anga lo doh spah yengba.*
- **Breakdown:** *Anga* [me] *lo* [to] *doh* [this] *spah* [goods] *yengba* [show/see]
- **English Translation:** Please let me see the goods.

#### Phrase 9: Confirming a Treaty or Deal
- **Wesean:** *Anga tem hao ktien kam to.*
- **Breakdown:** *Anga tem* [we] *hao* [good] *ktien* [agreement] *kam* [do/make] *to* [PAST]
- **English Translation:** We have successfully reached a business agreement.

#### Phrase 10: Environmental Quality Statement
- **Wesean:** *Doh di-nai hao sngi.*
- **Breakdown:** *doh* [this] *di-nai* [river water] *hao* [good/clean] *sngi* [day/today]
- **English Translation:** The river water is very clean today.

#### Phrase 11: Future Sales Projection
- **Wesean:** *Anga anga ke tang die po.*
- **Breakdown:** *Anga* [I] *anga* [my] *ke* [possessive] *tang* [rice] *die* [sell] *po* [FUT]
- **English Translation:** I am going to sell my harvest tomorrow.

#### Phrase 12: Administrative Record-Keeping
- **Wesean:** *Doh laika lo thoh hao.*
- **Breakdown:** *doh* [this] *laika* [book] *lo* [in] *thoh* [write] *hao* [well/please]
- **English Translation:** Please sign your name in this record book.

#### Phrase 13: Labor Force Audit
- **Wesean:** *Aei tanii lem lo terhu?*
- **Breakdown:** *aei* [how many] *tanii* [people] *lem* [field] *lo* [in] *terhu* [work]
- **English Translation:** How many people are currently working in the rice field?

#### Phrase 14: Resource Acquisition
- **Wesean:** *Anga tem nari bre zaw.*
- **Breakdown:** *Anga tem* [we] *nari* [iron/metal] *bre* [buy] *zaw* [need]
- **English Translation:** We need to purchase some iron tools.

#### Phrase 15: Cooperative Infrastructure Project
- **Wesean:** *Nam tem hao yum leh po.*
- **Breakdown:** *Nam* [village] *tem* [PL/community] *hao* [new] *yum* [house] *leh* [make/build] *po* [FUT]
- **English Translation:** The village community is going to build a new meeting hall.

## 3. PHONOLOGY & DIGITAL WRITING SYSTEM

For Wesean to succeed as a modern trade vehicle, it must be easy to type on basic mobile devices, smartphones, and computers without requiring specialized keyboard layouts, accents, or tonal indicators.

### Orthography (The Digital Alphabet)
Wesean uses a standardized Roman script containing 20 basic Latin characters. This standard Segmental Alphabet eliminates all diacritics, superscripts, and subscripts.

- **Vowels:** `a`, `e`, `i`, `o`, `u`
  - Vowels represent pure monophthongs: `a` [a], `e` [e], `i` [i], `o` [o], `u` [u].
  - Long vowels are represented by repeating the vowel letter (e.g., *khaa*).
- **Consonants:** `b`, `d`, `g`, `h`, `k`, `l`, `m`, `n`, `ng`, `p`, `r`, `s`, `t`, `w`, `y`
  - All consonants represent their standard phonetic equivalents.
  - The digraph `ng` represents the velar nasal sound [ŋ] as in English *sing*.

### Pronunciation Rules
1. **The Penultimate Stress Rule:** Stress is invariant and falls exclusively on the second-to-last (penultimate) syllable of any polysyllabic word.
   - *yéng-ba* (YENG-ba)
   - *le-khá-ma* (le-KHA-ma)
   - *di-á-lo* (di-A-lo)
2. **Glottal Stops:** Regional glottal stops are neutralized in standard digital writing and speech. The double vowel spelling (e.g., *khaa*) represents a lengthened vowel, making speech clear without requiring custom typographic characters.
3. **Tonal Neutrality:** All tones from parent Tibeto-Burman languages are fully omitted. Words are spoken with a neutral, non-tonal pitch accent.

---

## 4. STEP-BY-STEP FLUENCY CURRICULUM

This 4-week self-study curriculum is structured to guide a beginner to conversational and business fluency. The focus is on real-world economic, digital, and cooperative applications.

### Week 1: Alphabet, Pronunciation & Simple SOV
- **Objective:** Master the digital alphabet, apply the penultimate stress rule, and construct basic present-tense sentences.
- **Focus Areas:**
  - Standard pronunciation of vowels and the `ng` sound.
  - Practicing stress shifts on compound words.
  - Memorizing pronouns (*anga*, *nang*, *paw*) and the plural marker *tem*.
  - Constructing simple Subject-Object-Verb sentences without tense particles.
- **Daily Exercises:** Write 5 sentences in the present tense describing daily actions (e.g., *Anga nang yengba.*).

### Week 2: Marketplace, Numbers & Basic Trading
- **Objective:** Learn numerals, understand currency/transaction terms, and negotiate basic trade prices.
- **Focus Areas:**
  - Foundational numbers (*it*, *ni*, *lai*, *ar*, etc.).
  - Marketplace vocabulary (*iew*, *bre*, *die*, *dor*, *qian*, *sel*).
  - Asking for prices (*aei*) and stating quantities (e.g., *lai kilo tang*).
  - Using the past tense particle **to** (placed post-verbally) to confirm transactions.
- **Daily Exercises:** Roleplay a marketplace transaction, writing out the buyer and seller dialogue in Wesean.

### Week 3: Digital Commerce, Messaging & Locatives
- **Objective:** Apply Wesean to digital environments, mobile texting, and spatial movement.
- **Focus Areas:**
  - Typing efficiently on mobile keyboards without diacritics.
  - Master the locative post-positions **lo** (at/in/to) and **tin** (from) to state location or origin.
  - Using the liquid prefix **di-** rule to discuss resources, water quality, and agricultural logistics.
  - Utilizing the future particle **po** (placed post-verbally) to make plans and arrange logistics.
- **Daily Exercises:** Write short text-message style notes in Wesean coordinating a delivery of goods from the farm to the town market.

### Week 4: Synthesis, Agreement Writing & Governance
- **Objective:** Draft formal contracts, write business agreements, and negotiate cooperative projects.
- **Focus Areas:**
  - Using descriptors (*hao* for good/valid/safe) to qualify business terms.
  - Expressing consensus and contracts using the word *ktien* (voice/agreement).
  - Structuring complex, multi-clause logical thoughts using the negative particle **ma**.
  - Writing formal cooperative statements representing community projects.
- **Daily Exercises:** Write a 5-sentence cooperative agreement in Wesean detailing a joint agricultural project, describing the work (*terhu*), resources (*spah*), and final profit sharing (*qian*).

---

## 5. THE COMPREHENSIVE DICTIONARY (3,000 Entries)

The following dictionary contains 3,000 compound terms systematically generated using parent roots from the 13 Wesean tribes. 

*Etymology distribution follows the 13-tribe matrix: Boro-Garo 12.99%, Zo 12.99%, Meitei 10.00%, Jingphaw 10.00%, Khasi 9.00%, Angami 9.00%, Tani 7.99%, Konyak 7.00%, Karbi 7.00%, Tangkhul 4.01%, Ao 4.01%, Rongmei 4.01%, Mishmi 2.00%.*

---
### Numerals & Counting

| Wesean Term | Etymological Breakdown | English Definition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| gyat | Ta: *gyat* [one/digit] | eight (8) |
| gyatpak | Ta: *gyat* [digit] + Ji: *pak* [hundred-unit] | eight hundred (800) |
| gyatsom | Ta: *gyat* [digit] + Zo: *som* [ten-unit] | eightty (80) |
| it | Ta: *it* [one/digit] | one (1) |
| itmang | Ta: *it* [one] + BG: *mang* [vast] | countless; innumerable |
| itpak | Ta: *it* [digit] + Ji: *pak* [hundred-unit] | one hundred (100) |
| itsom | Ta: *it* [one] + Zo: *som* [ten-unit] | ten (10) |
